# Kimigayo Compiler Plan

Current plan only: position, milestone order, completion conditions, next actions and open issues. Update when position, next actions or blockers change, under [AGENTS Documentation](../../AGENTS.md#documentation). Required behavior is in [SPEC.md](../SPEC.md); implemented support is in [STATUS.md](../STATUS.md). Commits and artifacts hold unit evidence; [PLAN_HISTORY.md](PLAN_HISTORY.md) preserves selected historical summaries and is maintained only on explicit request. Items deferred by [Appendix D](../spec/appendices/D-deferred-features.md) are not planned here.

## 1. Scope

Implement the finalized language of SPEC.md (Chapters 1–19 and 22) and the implementation contracts of [IMPL.md](../IMPL.md) (Chapters 20–21, the test execution profile and Appendix A) for the Windows x64 profile, excluding what Appendix D defers; ObjectCallCompatible stage 2 (Appendix D.2) is in scope. Work follows unfinished implementation items and their dependencies. Milestone Programs require explicit user instruction as implementation targets; existing program results remain evidence, not stopping conditions.

- **Generics:** the initial profile monomorphizes (§21.3.1). Generic code sharing is deferred.
- **Origins and the Contract/type system** (§8, §15) remain complete requirements; implementation limits never narrow them.
- **Async tasks and workers:** implementation is deferred by user instruction; the preserved plan is Appendix D. Remaining callable effect-bound integration is G78.
- **Adopted library contracts:** channel delivery is FIFO by successful-send commit order, and remaining items are completely destroyed one at a time in reverse order (SPEC §22.3.4). Apply these when the deferred async implementation resumes. Slice `firstIndexWhere(predicate:)` remains unimplemented within the existing library support boundary.
- **Proposal text changes** and **NativeAOT tests** need explicit instruction. Instructed intake or closure includes register updates and moves under AGENTS.

## 2. Working rules

Work policy (commit and push per unit, a final Session per working session) is in [AGENTS.md](../../AGENTS.md); design and review in [WORKFLOW.md](WORKFLOW.md); checks and evidence in [VERIFICATION.md](VERIFICATION.md); structure rules, migration rules and the stage ledger in [COMPILER_ARCHITECTURE.md](COMPILER_ARCHITECTURE.md). PLAN holds current work and keeps explicit blockers with their next steps. A closed §7 row is deleted, its one-line summary kept in the landing commit; [PLAN_HISTORY](PLAN_HISTORY.md#closed-open-issues) changes only on an explicit request (AGENTS' update table; the owner, 2026-11-13, superseding 2026-11-01's 「完了済みの行を PLAN_HISTORY に要約して移す」); its open remainder stays here.

## 4. Milestones (execution order)

States: TODO / IN_PROGRESS / DONE. A milestone is DONE only when every condition in §5 holds. IDs follow the adopted implementation progression; [former IDs](../../tests/milestones/README.md#implementation-order) identify pre-2026-10-15 evidence. Broader coverage may follow an earlier program that uses a supported subset.

P44's MIR code generation remains pending. Other open remainders: G10, G65, G75, G78 and G79; the [program catalog](../../tests/milestones/README.md#implementation-order) owns completed-program status.

| P39 | Exclusive objects and views | IN_PROGRESS | The authored target still fails Binding on flow refinement; inherited field projections and the unchanged target harness remain. Refinement follows SPEC §14.10.1–§14.10.2 with Target completion (§13.6.5); `obj`/`objuniq` become invariant in their View Target (§15.3.5, Binding.TypeRelations). A bare Type-parameter or associated-Type `is` target becomes a `Language` error (`InvalidTypeFormation_Kd`, §13.6.4) and written Origins are rejected at any depth; Owned-proven generic targets such as `Slot<T>` stay `Unsupported_Kd` until runtime identity of instantiated Type arguments is implemented. |

## 5. Completion conditions (every program milestone)

1. The spec-derived program source builds unchanged with the selected compiler configuration (Release by default; Debug explicitly) and runs at O0 and O2 with the expected stdout, exit code and stderr.
2. `src/backend/windows-x64/test-milestone<N>.ps1` exists and passes: original source only, one build/direct run per O0/O2; dedicated feature tests cover behavior and required rejection diagnostics.
3. Session verification passes: zero warnings, the full suite for the selected compiler configuration, and the harnesses of all earlier completed programs.
4. The owning SPEC sections exercised by the program have focused positive and negative tests; unsupported neighboring forms fail with a diagnostic, never with wrong code.
5. STATUS.md describes the new support boundary, and the milestone is marked DONE here and in `tests/milestones/README.md`.

A program source is re-spelled only for a specification change or for an authoring correction, where the source contradicts its own specification-derived output. `tests/milestones/README.md` records each re-spelling with its cause; a completed program keeps DONE when conditions 1–3 hold for the re-spelled source.

Features that a program's source does not use belong to the milestone that owns them, not to that program.

## 6. Next actions

1. **Alternate timed targets (after R6; until then only wrong-code fixes):** the U7 remainder (§4's U7 row), then the P38 completion review (user decision). Exclusive receiver proofs follow P42 → OCC-X. Retain Unsupported guards until the common plan passes analysis and execution; continue independent work when blocked.
2. **Callable and collections:** complete G10/G65 remainders and the G75 residual boundaries. Keep ranking independent of bodies, erasure incomparable with other adaptations and selected failures without fallback. Dictionary pair access remains separate from completed P34.
3. **Verification and diagnostics:** migrate raw allocation measurements only when touched or recurring, preserving counts/order and failures. Carry earlier Move/Loan evidence and distinguish condition/Place identities. Review [inherited audit findings](DIAGNOSTICS.md#11-audit-findings-d1), including Note omissions and range-specific cases; no old audit item is closed merely by this documentation cleanup. Manual VS Code Quick Fix inspection and malformed-attribute recovery remain open.
4. **Effects:** generic-call summaries and caller static-Loan comparisons remain G78/STATUS limits. Static storage and shutdown belong to P42.
5. **Objects and FFI:** U8 implements nonnull handle Options before P41; T2 retains inferred C-layout diagnostics. P39 owns refinement and obj/objuniq invariance; P41 requires the Weak variance exception; P42 owns two-phase shutdown, static Abort codes and static Place roots before OCC-X/P43.
6. **Code rules and small context:** after analyzer retirement (§3; DECISIONS CR-R1), retain structural/size review obligations, ArchitectureRulesTest and entry-file contracts (AGENTS, DECISIONS Q1).

Concurrent sessions use separate worktrees and stage only their own paths. Library bodies reached only through generic dispatch are collected through the instance collector's Witness targets (`InstanceCollector.Callees`, `TyCtxt.Witness`; InstanceCollectorTest's K 4.5 rows); retain that path when selecting verification.

(G317) Pre-existing: an explicit capture list stops at its first failed entry and skips the later entries and the body (SPEC 23.1.2): in `[t@move, x@move]` x's independent Unsupported_Kd is missing, in `[t@move] () -> i32 => missing` the body's UnresolvedBinding_Kd is missing; a dependent first entry leaves the closure no record of its own. Cause: an entry is a `CaptureKoto` record struct, not a node. **Next:** entries become nodes (Parsing), each with its own failure and dependency derivation; the list and body continue. (G533) Found by G513's search and review (pre-existing; SPEC 8.11.1, 23.1.2): an effect-bound selector's Type arguments lose their own records, in two halves. (a) With `contract Take<T>` and `contract QuietTake: Take<i32>`, `effect Take<Missing>.take confined` (right arity) and `effect Take<i32, Missing>.take confined` report only InvalidEffectBound_Kd: `Binding.EffectTarget` (Binding.EffectDeclarations.cs) rejects the bound (`EffectRejection.NotReference`) when the argument list fails, instead of resting on the argument's record (probes f03, e01). (G535) Found by G513's review (pre-existing; SPEC 23.1.2, 10.5): a function reference's explicit Type arguments after the first failed one are not bound. `BindExplicitReferenceName` (Binding.Names.cs) returns `CompleteDependent(reference, syntax)` at the first failed argument, so with `func g<A, B>() -> i32 => 1`, `let h = g<Missing1, Missing2>` reports only `Missing1` (the call form `g<Missing1, Missing2>()` reports both). Bind every argument, then rest on the first failure (probe h01-fnref-two-missing). R5-M55's zero-sized Field/struct reporting is unchanged. Invalid by SPEC 8.2.3: both are identity premises naming complete Types, so i32 and bool would be identical by transitivity; their heads are known and differ, so the identity is Refuted, a Language error at the declaration ("Premise errors" row 1). G615's design (D-147) found the cause: no component implements SPEC 8.2.3's relation; `Binding.IdentityPremise` substitutes only and takes a class's first complete Type, so none of the four "Premise errors" rows is decided on dev. e1-e10 (the function, struct and chain forms, `T is ref/i32` with `T is i32`, `Option<i32>` against `Option<bool>`, and SPEC's own `conflict`, `cyclic` and `otherView`) get no premise error: nine are accepted, and the `when` form gives only `IncompatibleContractImplementation_Kd`. SPEC's valid `same` example (v4) is rejected with `TypeMismatch_Kd`, the same cause. No wrong code runs: a call to a contradicting declaration is `NoApplicableOverload_Kd`, so no instance is generated. The design's §4 rows become R7-C3's regression rows; the Front decided no interim check, which would be a second identity relation. (G704, the owner's O-141 (a)) Since G704 resolves `Never`, `T is Never` with a second, conflicting identity premise is accepted, G615's known limit, named so: f3 (`T is i32`), f3d (`T is string`), f3g (`T is Option<i32>`), f3h (`T is ()`), and with a body using the value g4r (`T is Never`, `T is i32`, `return a + 1`) and c5 (the two premises reversed); R7-C3a's identity classes reject them: one `InvalidConstraint_Kd` at f in either order and no record at the body's uses (`ConstraintBindingTest.NeverWithAConflictingIdentityPremiseIsAPremiseError`). No instance of a contradicting declaration can be made (calls give `NoApplicableOverload_Kd` or `UnsatisfiedConstraint_Kd`). (G722) Inside `contract Q: R, P<i32>, P<Self.(R).X>` with `Self.(R).X is i32`, the Contract's shape keeps two ancestors, two `value`s and two `Item`s (`BuildContract` dedups by interned reference in `PrepareContracts`, before `BindConstraints` binds the premise), so `effect P<i32>.value` and `effect P<Self.(R).X>.value` name two ancestors, a short `Item` in Q, a struct's unqualified `associate Item` and `x.value(1)` under `T is Q` are ambiguous: valid programs rejected (SPEC 8.9.1 step 1, 8.9.2). The struct form (`Self is Q`, one `value(x: i32)`) fails in the judge: `SameConformanceType` proves `P<Self.X>` = `P<i32>` but not `P<i32>` = `P<Self.X>` in the overlap scope (SPEC 8.2.3: identity is symmetric). C3 merges a Contract's ancestor paths under its own premises after they are bound and makes the judge side-independent; a merge now would read a direction-dependent judge (G615's lesson: no interim identity relation); probes valid e01, x06, a01, a03, g01, g02, x07; twins a01i, a03i, g02i, s01-s04. (H1) under an identity premise on the callee's own parameter, `SlotSolution.Pattern`'s `ContractType(pattern, caller scope)` rewrites the slot away (`func f<U>(u: U)` with `U is i32` and `f(u@move)`, `f(1@i32)`; a method's own `U`; `Box.init(other@move)` in a member with `T is i32`); (H2) without a premise, `Infer`'s equal-Type shortcut binds no slot for a composite parameter (`f<U>(u: ref/U)` with `f(u)`, `(U, U)`). Both give `UnboundTypeArgument_Kd`; no invalid probe is accepted; explicit Type arguments or an annotated result are the workaround. A local patch of both sites moves a valid explicit call to rejected (the experiment in the triage), so the fix is the slot's representation: a solve's variables in the instantiated signature (R7-I2's variables with R7-P3 a's signature view), with G711's twins as I1/P1 rows. Open: whether I2 c and P3 a instantiate the signature with variables (the Front's design question); if not, G711 needs its own unit. Also (G719's design review 2, N1): inside a struct's own declaration, a construction whose solution is the struct's own parameter (`Box.init(v)` with `v: T` in a member of `Box<T>`) is seeded on its re-run as open, since the call's slot seeding cannot tell that solution from an open own slot; dev's `InitializeCallSlots` had the same rule. (H3, D-1225) Polymorphic recursion with an inferred growing argument (SPEC 8.15.3, 10.8): `func g<T>(x: ref/T, ...)` with `let rx = x@ref` and `g(rx, ...)` (T_call = `ref/T`) gives `UnboundTypeArgument_Kd` at the call, as H2's `g(x, ...)` does. Traced: the argument is adapted at `FixedPattern.Of`'s pattern, whose open slot is encoded as the callee's own parameter, the body's `T` (Binding.CallSlots.cs). So `ref/ref/T` is adapted to `ref/T` (Binding.ArgumentKinds.Evidence.cs), and Infer's equal shortcut (Binding.CallSlots.cs) binds no slot. At a bare slot (`depth(o@move, ...)` with `o: Option<T>`) and at another callee the same argument is accepted. | (G457) BU; (G711) Front (Binding); G711 R7 plan: R7-P3 (plan revision 69 (B)) |

| G819 | Binding's own case enumerators skip a pair binder that admits no case (G750's remainder; found by the Middle's G723 MIR design, C-288, 2026-11-13). `Binding.CaseFact`, `CaseDigits`, `AppendProduct`, `AppendCases` and `AcquisitionCases` skip such a binder and enumerate the other binders' cases. That is the reading G750 removed from `CheckCaseBound`, `BindCaseAdaptation` and `FollowablePair`. They read any bound scope, a no-case definition's parts included, so MIR's contract (a published body's binders admit at least one case) does not cover them. Deleting their `Resolved` test alone would make `CaseDigits` divide by zero. Proposed: each reads `CaseProduct` 0 as no case, the one fact G750 publishes, and then `PairBinder.Resolved` has no reader. One of them reads MIR views: `Compilation.Publication.CaseFact` renders a MIR problem's views through `Binding.CaseFact`, `AppendProduct`, `AppendCases` and `CaseDigits`, which decode the view bits with Binding's own copy of `MirCases.Select`'s numbering (outer to inner, the last binder fastest) and its skip of a binder that admits no case. On that path the bodies are validated (G723: every binder admits a case), so the skip is dead there and the two numberings agree; a change to these enumerators keeps `Select`'s view numbering. Designed after G750 lands. | Front (BU); after R7's exit (plan revision 69 (B)) |

(a) Dependent records: with fewer written items than parameters, a value call publishes `NoApplicableOverload_Kd` beside the syntax Error: `f(null spare@uniq)` and `f(spare@uniq null)` at `put`, `f(1 2)` at `(i32, i32) -> ()` for a Function value, a Closure or a parameter, `action(1 2)` under `Callable<(i32, i32) -> i32>`; the Case construction `E.A(1 2)` publishes `TypeMismatch_Kd`. (b) A lost independent record: `pi(1 2, 3, 4)` loses its `NoApplicableOverload_Kd`, which `f(1 2, 3, 4)` and `E.A(1 2, 3, 4)` keep (more written items than parameters fail under every repair). Proposed change: `Binding.ArgumentMapping` publishes with each `CallArgumentMap` the recovered item that a failed map rests on. That is when the only failure is parameters left unsupplied and an argument is a recovery, which may stand for the missing items. DIAGNOSTICS 4.4's "the call's overload selection ... rests on the Error" gains the count's two directions (a D request with the unit). Removes the records of (a), restores (b), removes the second count rule and the routes' incidental dependence. Positions of items after a recovered item are the unit design's question. Twins: the triage's §1 (b1, b2, c1, c1n, c1c, c1p, c1g, c1e, c8_d; controls c1_d, c2, c3, c5, c8, c8e, c9). | Front (Binding); unscheduled; R7 plan: R7-P3 (plan revision 69 (B)) |

A call variable whose lower bounds are a signature Origin and a body-local Borrow's region takes no candidate (found by the Middle's RP17 of G1128, Q-618, C1; rows t-c1-loc and t-c1-loc-e, labels from SPEC first): the call solve's contravariant row does not read SPEC 15.6.1's rule that outliving a finite Origin is Proven, so it finds no strongest lower bound among `a` and the local region, and the call is this row's located limit. t-c1-loc, valid (T = `ref/i32 during a`, returned `during a`): `func app2<T>(g: (T) -> i32, h: (T) -> i32) -> T => $abort("no")` / `func mk<U>(x: U) -> (U) -> i32 => $abort("no")` / `func u(w: ref/i32 during a, g: (ref/i32 during a) -> i32) -> ref/i32 during a` / `    let z: i32 = 1` / `    let k = mk(z@ref)` / `    return app2(g@move, k@move)` gives two located Unsupported_Kd; t-c1-loc-e, invalid (both lower bounds local regions, `k = mk(z@ref)`, `m = mk(y@ref)`, `return app2(k@move, m@move)`, returned `during a`), gives the same two Unsupported_Kd where dev gave two UnsatisfiedOriginRelation_Kd (`the borrow y/z outlives a, which is false`) beside an Unsupported_Kd (a record difference under Q13: the Language records rest on the limit). | G1217 | A capturing closure literal in an inferred Property's initializer keeps its body in the header frame, so a re-entry of the Property's header through that body gives the interim `CyclicBinding_Kd`, a Language record on a valid program (the owner's Q13 rule 2 exception, O-472 (a) widened by O-475 (a); DECISIONS BU-237; G812 rulings 174-182; D-1007). Example (cap7v, valid by SPEC 7.7.2 and 11.6: the environment is the list, and typing a needs no Type of b): `group V` / `let a = match Option.Some(1)` / `Option.Some(let x) => (func [var x] () -> i32 => x + V.b)()` / `.None => 0` / `let b = a`; the class covers a written non-empty list and no list with a binding visible at the literal that its body can capture (cap1v, cap2v, cap3v, n3v, lt3v and their other-order twins; rows `BindingTest.ACapturingLiteralKeepsTheInterimCycleUntilItsCapturesArePublished`). dev rejects every one too (a fact-less `UnprovenConstraint_Kd` at the other member, or with the other member first a cause-less `PrerequisiteUnavailable_Kd` at the body's first use). Cause: a capturing literal's call receiver comes from its body (SPEC 7.7.3: a move of a capture makes the call Consuming), and with no list its captures come from its body's names, which the binder forms only by binding the body. Fix (removal condition of the exception): the literal's captures (from its body's names, by the binder's own lookup rule factored out first) and its receiver mode published without binding its body, so it joins the header frame's deferral; expected: the class accepted, mv3v accepted, wr4i keeps `TypeMismatch_Kd`. | Front (Binding, `Binding.Captures`, `Binding.Closures` `TypedByHeader`; the receiver mode with the Middle's `Binding.ClosureReceivers`) |

In `group V / let a = match Option.Some(1) / Option.Some(let x) => (func () => x + V.b)() / .None => 0 / let b = a` (cap1t; invalid by SPEC 11.6: the literal's result comes from its body, 7.7.1, which needs V.b's Type, b's is a's), dev gives `CyclicBinding_Kd` at `let a` with three cause-less `PrerequisiteUnavailable_Kd` and `InternalInvariant_Kd` at `x` (3:43) (ruling 173 (a)'s early return, which hid it, was removed by ruling 174 (2)). Cause: `x + V.b` fails on V.b, so no operation takes the capture use `x`, and `ClosureReceivers` lists it in `Unlisted`, which `BindConcreteClosure` fails with `BindingFailure.Internal` (R7-M53's net). | G1221 | A selection's in-place read is decided before its use, so a receiver on a compared string operand's path has two writers (SPEC 4.7.1 step 2: "The use determines the capability"; 13.4; 3.4.3; G147 rulings 55, 58, 60; DECISIONS BU-243; D-1013). One form therefore publishes two reads: `t.0` in `t.0.name == "b"` is SharedBorrow `ref/Point`, in `let n = t.0.name@ref` ReferenceRead `uniq/Point` (`t = pair@uniq`, element `uniq/(uniq/Point)`). One condition decided at the selection was measured and rejected (option (b), ruling 60). It loses an exclusive call receiver through layers, because AdaptInput takes a receiver's prepared reference only from a ReferenceRead: valid `t.0.mutate()` and `arr[i].mutate()` get `NoApplicableOverload_Kd`. Not a soundness defect, no wrong code on any probe. **Remedy:** each use (a comparison, a call's receiver adaptation, a borrow, a write) records a selection's read once, with its own capability; the selection publishes only the path's facts (the through-layers reference and the element facts). **Rows:** cp1 (V, compared) and ow3 (V, the same receiver outside a comparison); ox1 (V, `t.0.mutate()`) and ox4 (V, `arr[i].mutate()`); ox8 (I, `let n = t.0.name@ref; t.0.mutate()`: `CallActivationConflict_Kd` at `t.0.mutate()`); ow2 (V, `t.0.name == t.0.name`, two inspections of one Place); r2d3o (V, an object-case user index on a compared path, `var entries: Dictionary<i32, obj/Point> = [1: Point.init()@obj]; return entries[1].name == "b"`: the selection writes `ref/(obj/Point)`, the comparison `objref/(obj/Point)`; accepted today); and the predicted fault shape (to be written and labelled from SPEC before its run): a member receiver below several layers, one of them a pair layer admitting only owner (SPEC 13.5.5: it selects the Place itself), on a compared path. There ReceiverThroughLayers' reference takes the receiver Place's own Origin, which names a Projection atom, so by reading the census's extras check would fault while two writers remain (not run). **Exit:** CompareInPlace's second write of a receiver removed, with every row keeping its label. | Front (Binding) |

| G1239 | Two record defects at a generic nested enum's construction (low class, not R7's class 1; found by PLAN G924 AF-3's Case-path supplement and its review; probes and labels in `front\notes\ArgFlow\af3\rcp\`; D-1031). (1) **The inferred-construction flag is set by binding order, not by form:** `inferredConstructionTargets` holds a nested enum's qualifier with an omitted own list only when its first binding in the pass had no expected Type of the same enum (`ConstructionTargetType`, Binding.ContainerReferences.cs), so an argument, which is probed first, sets it (`kn1(Outer<i32>.Slot.Some(1))`, f5a), and the same construction in a `let` with an expectation does not (`let o: Outer<i32>.Slot<i64> = Outer<i32>.Slot.Some(1)`, f5b). Both are accepted, with no acceptance or IR difference (f5a-f5f), but it is observable in records: with `Slot<U>` declared `U is Mark` and `i64` not `Mark`, the `let` form (f5d, flag unset) reports the failed Constraint also at the qualifier `Outer<i32>.Slot` (11:35), because `ValidateConstraintUses` skips the qualifier only when the flag is set; the argument form (f5c) has no such record. Three prove the occurrence's Type once more and read no failed input directly: (1) ValidateConstraintUses at a declaration node whose BoundType is its written Type (a Function at its result, `func g() -> Box<S>`: 7:6 beside 7:13; a Field's name, `var x: Box<S>`: 8:16); (2) ValidateAccessor's formation of a stored Property's Type (`var x: Box<S>`, `var f: (i32) -> Box<S>`: the Property at 8:12; the Field formation check of G373, closed by R7-V1c; it runs before ValidateConstraintUses, BU-124's order); (3) CompleteEnumAcquisitions' formation of `.None`'s Type from its annotation (`let b: Option<Box<S>> = .None`: 8:29). The one-owner form for (1)-(3): a declaration reads its written Type's verdict (the binder that gives the declaration its Type publishes the written Type's node: no such relation exists, `WrittenType` reads a Type), the expected-Type acquisitions read the annotation's verdict, and ValidateAccessor's formation moves after ValidateConstraintUses with the Property's failure kept for `SuppliesNoEvidence` (a BU-124 revision). Programs (each `contract C`, `struct Box<T>` with `T is C`, `struct S` not C): `struct H` + `public var x: Box<S>` (records at 8:12, 8:16, 8:19; one independent); `func g() -> Box<S> => $abort("no")` (7:6, 7:13); `let b: Option<Box<S>> = .None` (8:19, 8:29); probes t1, t2, t3, d2 (`front\notes\V1\design-v1c.md` §3.4, §4.2). A callee `ref/(ref/F)` or `ref/(uniq/F)` with two Callable signatures (loc-ref2, loc-refuniq) gives `NoApplicableOverload_Kd` "none of 0 candidates applies" while the one-signature twin is accepted; `f: s/F` with `s is owner or ref` (recv-pair, recv-pair2) gives `NotCallable_Kd`, for one signature too. Direction: one statement of the callee's Callable (the canonical layer walk) read by `TryCallable`, `SelectValueCall`, `CallableClosure` and `AcquireValueCallee`; until a case is implemented, the located `Unsupported_Kd`. Z3 publishes nothing for it. Rows: loc-ref2, loc-refuniq, recv-pair and recv-pair2 failing before; loc-ref2-one, recv-uniq and recv-own as guards. | G1353 | A native build's `StageInput` leaves a held staged copy to finalization when the temporary's delete throws on the reuse branch (found by the Middle's review of Tatara StagedHold, C-531 N-1, T's ruling; DECISIONS T-M41; D-1228): `NativeToolchain.Build.cs`, `StageInput`: `return held` passes through `finally { File.Delete(temporary); }`, so the build fails, but the read-sharing handle is never returned or disposed, and a long-lived process (`kimi tatara`, the LSP host) refuses foreign writers to that copy until the finalizer runs. **Proposed:** delete the temporary before holding, or dispose the held copy when the delete fails. | Tatara (the native build) |
